                    "constants": {
                        "0": "For a ₹1.65 crore resale flat in Noida, ",
                        "1": "don't pay a token or sign an unconditional agreement until you've verified ownership, the property's registry status, and all outstanding dues.",
                        "2": " A flat can look perfect and still have problems with its title, the land lease, unpaid society charges or transfer eligibility.",
                        "3": "I'd divide your checks into two parts: documents proving you can legally buy the flat, and charges that could become your responsibility after purchase.",
                        "4": "1. Documents you must verify",
                        "5": "1. Registered sale deed and ownership chain",
                        "6": "Ask for the seller's registered sale deed and previous title documents establishing the ownership chain. Confirm the seller's name, flat number, tower, floor, area and property identifiers match the actual flat. If the property was inherited, jointly owned or held through a power of attorney, get specialist legal advice on the additional documents required.",
                        "7": "2. Registry and Noida Authority records",
                        "8": "Establish whether the flat has a registered conveyance deed or sale deed, or whether it remains subject to a builder's allotment and transfer process. For a leasehold property, have your lawyer verify the lease deed, relevant authority permissions and transfer requirements. ",
                        "9": "An allotment letter alone is not equivalent to registered ownership.",
                        "10": "3. Encumbrance and loan checks",
                        "11": "Ask whether the flat has an outstanding home loan, mortgage, lien, court attachment or other restriction. Have a lawyer search the relevant registration records and available CERSAI records where applicable. If there is a loan, obtain the lender's outstanding statement and a documented process for repayment and release of the mortgage at closing.",
                        "12": "4. Occupancy and building approvals",
                        "13": "Request the applicable occupancy certificate (OC), completion certificate (CC), sanctioned building plan and evidence that the actual flat conforms to approved plans. Ask your lawyer to verify whether the project has any unresolved approval or deviation issues.",
                        "14": "5. Society and utility records",
                        "15": "Get a written no-dues certificate from the society or maintenance agency, recent maintenance bills, electricity and water bills, property-tax records where applicable, and confirmation of the allocated parking space. Check whether any special assessment or major repair levy has already been approved.",
                        "16": "6. Litigation and RERA checks",
                        "17": "Search for relevant disputes involving the flat, seller, builder and project. Check the project's RERA record where applicable, including disclosed completion details and complaints. A clean RERA page by itself does not establish clear title.",
                        "18": "2. Outstanding charges that could cost you extra",
                        "19": "Ask the seller and society for written statements, not just verbal assurances.",
                        "20": "Charge or liability",
                        "21": "What to ask for",
                        "22": "Noida Authority dues",
                        "23": "Any unpaid lease rent, land-related dues, transfer charges, interest or penalties",
                        "24": "Society maintenance",
                        "25": "Outstanding monthly maintenance, late fees and any approved special levy",
                        "26": "Property tax",
                        "27": "Latest paid receipts and confirmation of arrears, if applicable",
                        "28": "Electricity and water",
                        "29": "Final bills, unpaid amounts, meter details and any connection-related dues",
                        "30": "Home loan",
                        "31": "Current outstanding amount, lender's foreclosure statement and mortgage-release procedure",
                        "32": "Parking and transfer",
                        "33": "Whether parking is legally allocated and whether society or authority transfer fees apply",
                        "34": "Major repairs",
                        "35": "Any upcoming lift replacement, waterproofing, façade work, plumbing repairs or other major expenses",
                        "36": "Brokerage and documentation",
                        "37": "Who pays the broker, lawyer, deed drafting and other agreed transaction expenses",
                        "38": "Pay special attention to Noida Authority dues.",
                        "39": " Depending on the project's land and lease arrangements, an apartment's transfer process can involve authority-related documentation and charges. Your lawyer should establish exactly which approvals and payments apply to this property rather than relying on the seller's estimate.",
                        "40": "For UP registration procedures and applicable stamp duty, consult the official ",
                        "41": "25bfac6e-fbcd-5e68-a028-1e13eb99de53",
                        "42": "IGRS Uttar Pradesh portal",
                        "43": ". ",
                        "44": [
                            "turn809613search11"
                        ],
                        "45": "624eac0f-78cb-533b-95ff-98c4c916401a",
                        "46": "3. Don't forget TDS on the ₹1.65 crore purchase",
                        "47": "If the seller is an Indian tax resident, Section 194-IA generally requires you, as the buyer, to deduct ",
                        "48": "1% TDS on the consideration or stamp-duty value, whichever is higher",
                        "49": ", subject to the statutory rules. At ₹1.65 crore, 1% of the quoted price alone is ₹1.65 lakh, assuming the stamp-duty value is not higher and no other applicable adjustment changes the calculation. This is deducted from the amount paid to the seller; it is not an extra ₹1.65 lakh added to the purchase price. If the seller is non-resident, different tax provisions apply. ",
                        "50": [
                            "turn809613search0",
                            "turn809613search48"
                        ],
                        "51": "942d2c4a-6ecd-545a-a687-b8420b25b9e3",
                        "52": "Have your CA confirm the correct treatment of the transaction, including the applicable filing process for the date of purchase.",
                        "53": "4. Use this checklist before paying a token",
                        "54": "Resale purchase checklist",
                        "55": " of ",
                        "56": " Copy checklist",
                        "57": "My strongest advice is to appoint an independent Noida property lawyer who represents you, not the seller or broker. Have the lawyer review the documents and the draft sale agreement before any non-refundable payment.",
                        "58": "One detail would help me make this more specific: ",
                        "59": "Is the flat already registered in the seller's name, or does it still have only a builder allotment letter or transfer paperwork pending?",
                        "60": " That changes which documents and liabilities deserve the most attention."
                    },
                    "fallbackMarkdown": "For a ₹1.65 crore resale flat in Noida, **don't pay a token or sign an unconditional agreement until you've verified ownership, the property's registry status, and all outstanding dues.** A flat can look perfect and still have problems with its title, the land lease, unpaid society charges or transfer eligibility.\n\nI'd divide your checks into two parts: documents proving you can legally buy the flat, and charges that could become your responsibility after purchase.\n\n## 1\\. Documents you must verify\n\n**1\\. Registered sale deed and ownership chain**\n\nAsk for the seller's registered sale deed and previous title documents establishing the ownership chain. Confirm the seller's name, flat number, tower, floor, area and property identifiers match the actual flat. If the property was inherited, jointly owned or held through a power of attorney, get specialist legal advice on the additional documents required.\n\n---\n\n**2\\. Registry and Noida Authority records**\n\nEstablish whether the flat has a registered conveyance deed or sale deed, or whether it remains subject to a builder's allotment and transfer process. For a leasehold property, have your lawyer verify the lease deed, relevant authority permissions and transfer requirements. **An allotment letter alone is not equivalent to registered ownership.**\n\n---\n\n**3\\. Encumbrance and loan checks**\n\nAsk whether the flat has an outstanding home loan, mortgage, lien, court attachment or other restriction. Have a lawyer search the relevant registration records and available CERSAI records where applicable. If there is a loan, obtain the lender's outstanding statement and a documented process for repayment and release of the mortgage at closing.\n\n---\n\n**4\\. Occupancy and building approvals**\n\nRequest the applicable occupancy certificate (OC), completion certificate (CC), sanctioned building plan and evidence that the actual flat conforms to approved plans. Ask your lawyer to verify whether the project has any unresolved approval or deviation issues.\n\n---\n\n**5\\. Society and utility records**\n\nGet a written no-dues certificate from the society or maintenance agency, recent maintenance bills, electricity and water bills, property-tax records where applicable, and confirmation of the allocated parking space. Check whether any special assessment or major repair levy has already been approved.\n\n---\n\n**6\\. Litigation and RERA checks**\n\nSearch for relevant disputes involving the flat, seller, builder and project. Check the project's RERA record where applicable, including disclosed completion details and complaints. A clean RERA page by itself does not establish clear title.\n\n## 2\\. Outstanding charges that could cost you extra\n\nAsk the seller and society for written statements, not just verbal assurances.\n\n| Charge or liability | What to ask for |\n|--------|--------|\n| Noida Authority dues | Any unpaid lease rent, land-related dues, transfer charges, interest or penalties |\n| Society maintenance | Outstanding monthly maintenance, late fees and any approved special levy |\n| Property tax | Latest paid receipts and confirmation of arrears, if applicable |\n| Electricity and water | Final bills, unpaid amounts, meter details and any connection-related dues |\n| Home loan | Current outstanding amount, lender's foreclosure statement and mortgage-release procedure |\n| Parking and transfer | Whether parking is legally allocated and whether society or authority transfer fees apply |\n| Major repairs | Any upcoming lift replacement, waterproofing, façade work, plumbing repairs or other major expenses |\n| Brokerage and documentation | Who pays the broker, lawyer, deed drafting and other agreed transaction expenses |\n\n**Pay special attention to Noida Authority dues.** Depending on the project's land and lease arrangements, an apartment's transfer process can involve authority-related documentation and charges. Your lawyer should establish exactly which approvals and payments apply to this property rather than relying on the seller's estimate.\n\nFor UP registration procedures and applicable stamp duty, consult the official urlIGRS Uttar Pradesh portalhttps://www.igrsup.gov.in/\\. citeturn809613search11\n\n## 3\\. Don't forget TDS on the ₹1.65 crore purchase\n\nIf the seller is an Indian tax resident, Section 194-IA generally requires you, as the buyer, to deduct **1% TDS on the consideration or stamp-duty value, whichever is higher**, subject to the statutory rules. At ₹1.65 crore, 1% of the quoted price alone is ₹1.65 lakh, assuming the stamp-duty value is not higher and no other applicable adjustment changes the calculation. This is deducted from the amount paid to the seller; it is not an extra ₹1.65 lakh added to the purchase price. If the seller is non-resident, different tax provisions apply. citeturn809613search0turn809613search48\n\nHave your CA confirm the correct treatment of the transaction, including the applicable filing process for the date of purchase.\n\n## 4\\. Use this checklist before paying a token\n\n## Resale purchase checklist\n\nof\n\n[Copy checklist]\n\nMy strongest advice is to appoint an independent Noida property lawyer who represents you, not the seller or broker. Have the lawyer review the documents and the draft sale agreement before any non-refundable payment.\n\nOne detail would help me make this more specific: **Is the flat already registered in the seller's name, or does it still have only a builder allotment letter or transfer paperwork pending?** That changes which documents and liabilities deserve the most attention.",

        {
            "id": "5697a611-efb0-4462-bb58-178513ad8a14",
            "author": {
                "role": "assistant",
                "name": null,
                "metadata": {}
            },
            "create_time": 1792072365.93269,
            "update_time": 1792072380.098881,
            "content": {
                "content_type": "text",
                "parts": [
                    "At **₹1.58 crore, the resale flat is worth pursuing, but only if the renovation costs are controlled and the construction itself is sound.** The ₹7 lakh reduction from the earlier ₹1.65 crore asking price gives you some room, but it can disappear quickly if the flat needs new bathrooms, flooring, electrical work and plumbing.\n\nSince your overall budget is ₹1.8 crore and you care more about construction quality than clubhouse amenities, I'd judge this deal on the *total cost of getting a safe, comfortable home*, not just the negotiated price.\n\n## 1. Recalculate the total purchase cost\n\nI'll use illustrative estimates for now. We still need the flat's size, condition and actual renovation quotation to make this precise.\n\nFor a Noida resale, the UP government's official registration portal provides a stamp-duty calculator, so the final duty should be checked against the property's applicable valuation and your ownership arrangement. <Cite refs={[\"turn719967search0\",\"turn719967search1\"]}/>\n\n## 2. Three renovation scenarios\n\nThese estimates assume a 3BHK, with the scope depending on whether you're refreshing finishes or replacing major components. Recent Noida renovation guides put a basic refresh below a full renovation involving flooring, bathrooms, plumbing, electrical work and kitchen replacement. <Cite refs={[\"turn719967search5\",\"turn719967search6\"]}/>\n\n| Cost component | Light renovation | Standard renovation | Major renovation |\n|---|---:|---:|---:|\n| Purchase price | ₹1.58 Cr | ₹1.58 Cr | ₹1.58 Cr |\n| Stamp duty + registration* | ₹12.64 L | ₹12.64 L | ₹12.64 L |\n| Legal, brokerage and other costs** | ₹1.5 L | ₹1.5 L | ₹3 L |\n| Renovation allowance | ₹5 L | ₹12 L | ₹20 L |\n| Renovation contingency (15%) | ₹0.75 L | ₹1.8 L | ₹3 L |\n| **Estimated total** | **₹1.80 Cr** | **₹1.94 Cr** | **₹2.19 Cr** |\n| **Above your ₹1.8 Cr budget** | Approximately break-even | ₹14 L | ₹39 L |\n\n<text color=\"secondary\" size=\"xs\">*Assumes 8% combined stamp duty and registration on ₹1.58 crore. Actual charges can differ if the applicable stamp valuation exceeds the price or a concession applies. **Planning allowances, not verified quotations; brokerage and legal costs depend on the transaction. These scenarios exclude any undisclosed Authority or society arrears.</text>\n\n## 3. Is the deal worth pursuing?\n\nMy verdict: **Yes, pursue it conditionally, but don't commit until you have an itemised renovation quote.**\n\n- **If it needs only ₹4–5 lakh of cosmetic work:** It could fit your budget, but there's almost no room for surprise costs.\n- **If it needs ₹8–12 lakh of renovation:** I'd negotiate harder or increase your total budget. At ₹1.58 crore, the likely all-in cost is already above ₹1.8 crore.\n- **If it needs major plumbing, waterproofing or electrical replacement:** Get a qualified inspection before negotiating. If there is evidence of structural problems, don't treat renovation as a cosmetic expense.\n\nYour priority is construction quality, so don't spend your renovation budget hiding cracks or covering damp walls. Establish the cause and cost of repair first.\n\n## 4. What price should you negotiate?\n\nIf ₹1.8 crore is a hard all-in ceiling, my illustrative target would be:\n\n<box border radius=\"xl\" padding={3} gap={2}>\n  <text color=\"secondary\" weight=\"medium\" size=\"sm\">Target purchase price with standard renovation</text>\n  <title size=\"2xl\">₹1.45–1.48 crore</title>\n  <text color=\"secondary\" size=\"sm\">Assuming ₹12 lakh renovation, 15% contingency, 8% purchase duty/registration and ₹1.5 lakh in other costs.</text>\n</box>\n\nAt ₹1.58 crore, you could still make an offer, but I wouldn't justify it by saying the seller has already reduced the price. The right price is the one that leaves enough money to renovate properly and retain an emergency reserve.\n\n## 5. Tell me what actually needs renovation\n\n{@body const [size,setSize] = DIL.useState(\"1200–1500 sq ft\")}\n{@body const [work,setWork] = DIL.useState([\"Kitchen\",\"Bathrooms\",\"Flooring\"])}\n{@body const [budget,setBudget] = DIL.useState(\"₹8–12 lakh\")}\n{@body const [budgetHard,setBudgetHard] = DIL.useState(\"₹1.8 crore all-in\")}\n<box border radius=\"xl\" padding={4} gap={4}>\n  <box gap={1}>\n    <label>Approximate flat size</label>\n    <radio-group value={size} onChange={setSize} direction=\"col\">\n      <radio value=\"Under 1200 sq ft\">Under 1,200 sq ft</radio>\n      <radio value=\"1200–1500 sq ft\">1,200–1,500 sq ft</radio>\n      <radio value=\"Over 1500 sq ft\">Over 1,500 sq ft</radio>\n      <radio value=\"Unknown\">Not sure</radio>\n    </radio-group>\n  </box>\n  <box gap={2}>\n    <label>What needs work?</label>\n    {#each [\"Painting\",\"Flooring\",\"Kitchen\",\"Bathrooms\",\"Electrical wiring\",\"Plumbing / leakage\",\"Doors and windows\",\"Structural cracks or dampness\"] as item}\n      <checkbox key={item} checked={work.includes(item)} onChange={v=>setWork(old=>v?[...old,item]:old.filter(x=>x!==item))} lineThrough={false}>{item}</checkbox>\n    {/each}\n  </box>\n  <box gap={1}>\n    <label>Renovation estimate received so far</label>\n    <radio-group value={budget} onChange={setBudget} direction=\"col\">\n      <radio value=\"Under ₹5 lakh\">Under ₹5 lakh</radio>\n      <radio value=\"₹5–8 lakh\">₹5–8 lakh</radio>\n      <radio value=\"₹8–12 lakh\">₹8–12 lakh</radio>\n      <radio value=\"₹12–20 lakh\">₹12–20 lakh</radio>\n      <radio value=\"No quote yet\">No quote yet</radio>\n    </radio-group>\n  </box>\n  <box gap={1}>\n    <label>Your total budget</label>\n    <radio-group value={budgetHard} onChange={setBudgetHard} direction=\"col\">\n      <radio value=\"₹1.8 crore all-in\">₹1.8 crore, including everything</radio>\n      <radio value=\"₹1.8 crore plus emergency reserve\">₹1.8 crore plus a separate emergency reserve</radio>\n      <radio value=\"Can stretch above ₹1.8 crore\">I can stretch above ₹1.8 crore</radio>\n    </radio-group>\n  </box>\n  <button block onClick={()=>GenUI.issueNewTurn(\"Recalculate whether a Noida resale flat priced at ₹1.58 crore is worth pursuing. Size: \"+size+\". Renovation work needed: \"+(work.join(\", \")||\"not specified\")+\". Renovation estimate: \"+budget+\". Budget constraint: \"+budgetHard+\". Include stamp duty/registration assumptions, itemised renovation costs, contingency, all-in scenarios and a recommended maximum offer. Flag structural defects separately from cosmetic work.\")}>Recalculate my deal</button>\n</box>\n\n"
                ]
            },
            "status": "finished_successfully",
            "end_turn": true,
            "weight": 1.0,
            "metadata": {

                    "constants": {
                        "0": "At ",
                        "1": "₹1.58 crore, the resale flat is worth pursuing, but only if the renovation costs are controlled and the construction itself is sound.",
                        "2": " The ₹7 lakh reduction from the earlier ₹1.65 crore asking price gives you some room, but it can disappear quickly if the flat needs new bathrooms, flooring, electrical work and plumbing.",
                        "3": "Since your overall budget is ₹1.8 crore and you care more about construction quality than clubhouse amenities, I'd judge this deal on the ",
                        "4": "total cost of getting a safe, comfortable home",
                        "5": ", not just the negotiated price.",
                        "6": "1. Recalculate the total purchase cost",
                        "7": "I'll use illustrative estimates for now. We still need the flat's size, condition and actual renovation quotation to make this precise.",
                        "8": "For a Noida resale, the UP government's official registration portal provides a stamp-duty calculator, so the final duty should be checked against the property's applicable valuation and your ownership arrangement. ",
                        "9": [
                            "turn719967search0",
                            "turn719967search1"
                        ],
                        "10": "624eac0f-78cb-533b-95ff-98c4c916401a",
                        "11": "2. Three renovation scenarios",
                        "12": "These estimates assume a 3BHK, with the scope depending on whether you're refreshing finishes or replacing major components. Recent Noida renovation guides put a basic refresh below a full renovation involving flooring, bathrooms, plumbing, electrical work and kitchen replacement. ",
                        "13": [
                            "turn719967search5",
                            "turn719967search6"
                        ],

These scenarios exclude any undisclosed Authority or society arrears.",
                        "48": "3. Is the deal worth pursuing?",
                        "49": "My verdict: ",
                        "50": "Yes, pursue it conditionally, but don't commit until you have an itemised renovation quote.",
                        "51": "If it needs only ₹4–5 lakh of cosmetic work:",
                        "52": " It could fit your budget, but there's almost no room for surprise costs.",
                        "53": "If it needs ₹8–12 lakh of renovation:",
                        "54": " I'd negotiate harder or increase your total budget. At ₹1.58 crore, the likely all-in cost is already above ₹1.8 crore.",
                        "55": "If it needs major plumbing, waterproofing or electrical replacement:",
                        "56": " Get a qualified inspection before negotiating. If there is evidence of structural problems, don't treat renovation as a cosmetic expense.",
                        "57": "Your priority is construction quality, so don't spend your renovation budget hiding cracks or covering damp walls. Establish the cause and cost of repair first.",
                        "58": "4. What price should you negotiate?",
                        "59": "If ₹1.8 crore is a hard all-in ceiling, my illustrative target would be:",
                        "60": "Target purchase price with standard renovation",
                        "61": "₹1.45–1.48 crore",
                        "62": "Assuming ₹12 lakh renovation, 15% contingency, 8% purchase duty/registration and ₹1.5 lakh in other costs.",
                        "63": "At ₹1.58 crore, you could still make an offer, but I wouldn't justify it by saying the seller has already reduced the price. The right price is the one that leaves enough money to renovate properly and retain an emergency reserve.",
                        "64": "5. Tell me what actually needs renovation",
                        "65": "Approximate flat size",
                        "66": "Under 1,200 sq ft",
                        "67": "1,200–1,500 sq ft",
                        "68": "Over 1,500 sq ft",
                        "69": "Not sure",
                        "70": "What needs work?",
                        "71": "Renovation estimate received so far",

                        "75": "₹12–20 lakh",

                        "78": "₹1.8 crore, including everything",
                        "79": "₹1.8 crore plus a separate emergency reserve",
                        "80": "I can stretch above ₹1.8 crore",
                        "81": "Recalculate my deal"
                    },
                    "fallbackMarkdown": "At **₹1.58 crore, the resale flat is worth pursuing, but only if the renovation costs are controlled and the construction itself is sound.** The ₹7 lakh reduction from the earlier ₹1.65 crore asking price gives you some room, but it can disappear quickly if the flat needs new bathrooms, flooring, electrical work and plumbing.\n\nSince your overall budget is ₹1.8 crore and you care more about construction quality than clubhouse amenities, I'd judge this deal on the *total cost of getting a safe, comfortable home*, not just the negotiated price.\n\n## 1\\. Recalculate the total purchase cost\n\nI'll use illustrative estimates for now. We still need the flat's size, condition and actual renovation quotation to make this precise.\n\nFor a Noida resale, the UP government's official registration portal provides a stamp-duty calculator, so the final duty should be checked against the property's applicable valuation and your ownership arrangement. citeturn719967search0turn719967search1\n\n## 2\\. Three renovation scenarios\n\nThese estimates assume a 3BHK, with the scope depending on whether you're refreshing finishes or replacing major components. Recent Noida renovation guides put a basic refresh below a full renovation involving flooring, bathrooms, plumbing, electrical work and kitchen replacement. citeturn719967search5turn719967search6\n\n| Cost component | Light renovation | Standard renovation | Major renovation |\n|--------|--------|--------|--------|\n| Purchase price | ₹1.58 Cr | ₹1.58 Cr | ₹1.58 Cr |\n| Stamp duty + registration\\* | ₹12.64 L | ₹12.64 L | ₹12.64 L |\n| Legal, brokerage and other costs\\*\\* | ₹1.5 L | ₹1.5 L | ₹3 L |\n| Renovation allowance | ₹5 L | ₹12 L | ₹20 L |\n| Renovation contingency (15%) | ₹0.75 L | ₹1.8 L | ₹3 L |\n| **Estimated total** | **₹1.80 Cr** | **₹1.94 Cr** | **₹2.19 Cr** |\n| **Above your ₹1.8 Cr budget** | Approximately break-even | ₹14 L | ₹39 L |\n\n\\*Assumes 8% combined stamp duty and registration on ₹1.58 crore. Actual charges can differ if the applicable stamp valuation exceeds the price or a concession applies. \\*\\*Planning allowances, not verified quotations; brokerage and legal costs depend on the transaction. These scenarios exclude any undisclosed Authority or society arrears.\n\n## 3\\. Is the deal worth pursuing?\n\nMy verdict: **Yes, pursue it conditionally, but don't commit until you have an itemised renovation quote.**\n\n- **If it needs only ₹4–5 lakh of cosmetic work\\:** It could fit your budget, but there's almost no room for surprise costs.\n- **If it needs ₹8–12 lakh of renovation\\:** I'd negotiate harder or increase your total budget. At ₹1.58 crore, the likely all-in cost is already above ₹1.8 crore.\n- **If it needs major plumbing, waterproofing or electrical replacement\\:** Get a qualified inspection before negotiating. If there is evidence of structural problems, don't treat renovation as a cosmetic expense.\n\nYour priority is construction quality, so don't spend your renovation budget hiding cracks or covering damp walls. Establish the cause and cost of repair first.\n\n## 4\\. What price should you negotiate?\n\nIf ₹1.8 crore is a hard all-in ceiling, my illustrative target would be\\:\n\nTarget purchase price with standard renovation\n\n# ₹1.45–1.48 crore\n\nAssuming ₹12 lakh renovation, 15% contingency, 8% purchase duty/registration and ₹1.5 lakh in other costs.\n\nAt ₹1.58 crore, you could still make an offer, but I wouldn't justify it by saying the seller has already reduced the price. The right price is the one that leaves enough money to renovate properly and retain an emergency reserve.\n\n## 5\\. Tell me what actually needs renovation\n\nApproximate flat size\n\nUnder 1,200 sq ft\n\n1,200–1,500 sq ft\n\nOver 1,500 sq ft\n\nNot sure\n\nWhat needs work?\n\nRenovation estimate received so far\n\nUnder ₹5 lakh\n\n₹5–8 lakh\n\n₹8–12 lakh\n\n₹12–20 lakh\n\nNo quote yet\n\nYour total budget\n\n₹1.8 crore, including everything\n\n₹1.8 crore plus a separate emergency reserve\n\nI can stretch above ₹1.8 crore\n\n[Recalculate my deal]",
